Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of control or anything, because I know what I'm doing. But... yeah, once I start, it's almost like my hands just keep reaching for the next thing automatically.

Um... well, it's kind of a mix, I guess? I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just blindly grabbing at things like a wild animal or something... I do usually start out picking something specific that I want. 

But... I guess once I get going, it's more about just whatever is right there. Like, if I finish the chips, I might just move on to whatever is next in the pantry, even if it's not something I'd normally choose. Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to control it. But... I guess it's like, once I start, it feels like I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically?

I mean, I hope that answers your question okay.\n\nDoctor: During these eating episodes, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of control or anything, because I know what I'm doing. But... yeah, once I start, it's almost like my hands just keep reaching for the next thing automatically. \n\nLike, in my head, I'll be thinking, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" but... I just don't actually stop. Not until all the food is gone, or until my stomach hurts too much to keep going. I really try to stop, I promise I do, but it's just... hard in the moment. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I try not to step on the scale very often, just so I don't get too in my head about things, you know? But my work uniforms still fit exactly the same, so I'm pretty sure it's been stable. \n\nI mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two here or there, but definitely nothing significant at all. I hope it's okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I can try to start weighing myself if that's something you need me to keep track of...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really don't want you to think I'm completely helpless or anything... but, no, not really. \n\nLike I was saying, it just feels... automatic? Even when my brain is telling me to stop, like, \"Fumi, you really need to get back to your sketches,\" my hands just sort of keep going on their own. I don't really stop until everything is empty or my stomach is just too full and uncomfortable. I really do try to be in control, I promise. Is that okay? I mean, I hope that answers what you were asking without sounding too terrible...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during these episodes or is it more like you're eating whatever is available?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of a mix, I guess? I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just blindly grabbing at things like a wild animal or something... I do usually start out picking something specific that I want. \n\nBut... I guess once I get going, it's more about just whatever is right there. Like, if I finish the chips, I might just move on to whatever is next in the pantry, even if it's not something I'd normally choose. I try to make good choices, really I do, but sometimes it's just easier to grab what's close. I hope that makes sense? I'm sorry if my answers are a little confusing...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed after these eating episodes, in addition to feeling gross and disappointed in yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. I just... I feel so gross and disappointed in myself afterward. Like, I'm an adult, I should have more control over this, you know? \n\nI try to keep a positive attitude most of the time, I really do. But after it happens, I just get really down on myself. I actually... um... I end up writing some pretty awful things about myself in my journal when I feel like that. I know I shouldn't, and I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. I hope you don't think less of me for that... I just get so frustrated with myself. Does that sound like what you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worth as a person is tied to your eating habits or your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to think like that. I know my weight doesn't, like, define me or make me a bad person. \n\nI guess I just... I want to be healthy and do a good job at work and everything. I know I get upset with myself sometimes, but I don't think I'm worthless just because of how I eat. I try to focus on the good things, you know? Like my sketching and trying to be a good person. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed with my weight or anything like that... Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to control it. But... I guess it's like, once I start, it feels like I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically? \n\nEven in my head, I'll be telling myself, like, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" or I'll remind myself that I need to get up early for work. I really try to reason with myself. But... I don't usually actually stop until the food is gone or I just feel too uncomfortable to keep going. I know that sounds bad, and I'm really trying to work on it. I hope you don't think I'm just weak-willed... is it normal to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I don't want it to sound like I'm just stuffing my face or anything, but... I do tend to eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's usually when I'm just standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed and my sketchbook is still open next to me. I just... I eat really quickly, almost like I'm trying to get it all down before I have time to really think about what I'm doing. I barely even taste it, honestly. I know I should slow down and chew my food properly, and I'm really going to try to be better about that. I hope that doesn't make me sound too out of control... is it common for people to eat fast like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about these eating episodes throughout the day, even when you're not hungry?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really.     "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I mean, I really try to be in control. I don't want you to think I'm just completely letting go or not caring. I do care, a lot. 

But... well, during those times, I guess it feels like my control is just... slipping a little bit? Like, I'll tell myself I'm only going to have a little, and I really mean it when I say it. But then it just sort of happens, and before I know it, I've eaten more than I meant to. I try so hard to pull it back, I really do. I don't want to be someone who just can't control themselves. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... yeah, I guess it has been a while. I don't want it to sound like it's an everyday thing, because it's not! I really try to be good most of the time. 

But, um... for the past several months, it's usually like... maybe one night a week? I'll get off work—cleaning all day can be kind of tiring—and I'll stop at the store and just... buy a little too much, and then eat it all in one go. I really wish I didn't do it, and I always tell myself I'm going to stop. But I can't really remember a recent month where I went more than a week or so without it happening. I really am trying to break the habit, though. Is... is it normal for it to go on that long even when you're trying to stop?

I probably *should* exercise or something to make up for it, but honestly, after a long shift cleaning buildings, I'm usually just too tired. \n\nSo I just kind of... sit there and feel awful about it. And then I usually just go to sleep. Is that what you meant? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: How often do these eating episodes happen?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's not every single day, so I guess it's not too terrible. But... for the past several months, I'd say it happens usually at least one night a week. \n\nLike, I'll finish my shift and just buy way too much food on the way home, and then I end up eating it all in, like, one stretch. I keep thinking I'll snap out of it and do better, but... honestly, I can't really remember a recent month where it stopped for more than a week or so. \n\nI'm sorry, is that the kind of timeline you were looking for? It's more like I just sort of zone out, I guess? I just keep thinking about how I should have more control, you know? And that's usually when I end up pulling out my journal and writing all those awful things about myself. It's silly, I know, I probably shouldn't be so hard on myself, but... yeah, it definitely comes after. Is that normal? I mean, I hope that answers your question okay.\n\nDoctor: During these eating episodes, do you feel like you're unable to stop eating once you've started?\n\nPatient: Um... I guess maybe a little bit? I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm totally out of control or anything, because I know what I'm doing. But... yeah, once I start, it's almost like my hands just keep reaching for the next thing automatically. \n\nLike, in my head, I'll be thinking, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" but... I just don't actually stop. Not until all the food is gone, or until my stomach hurts too much to keep going. I really try to stop, I promise I do, but it's just... hard in the moment. Does that make sense? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: Um... well, no, not really. I try not to step on the scale very often, just so I don't get too in my head about things, you know? But my work uniforms still fit exactly the same, so I'm pretty sure it's been stable. \n\nI mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two here or there, but definitely nothing significant at all. I hope it's okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I can try to start weighing myself if that's something you need me to keep track of...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really don't want you to think I'm completely helpless or anything... but, no, not really. \n\nLike I was saying, it just feels... automatic? Even when my brain is telling me to stop, like, \"Fumi, you really need to get back to your sketches,\" my hands just sort of keep going on their own. I don't really stop until everything is empty or my stomach is just too full and uncomfortable. I really do try to be in control, I promise. Is that okay? I mean, I hope that answers what you were asking without sounding too terrible...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during these episodes or is it more like you're eating whatever is available?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of a mix, I guess? I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just blindly grabbing at things like a wild animal or something... I do usually start out picking something specific that I want. \n\nBut... I guess once I get going, it's more about just whatever is right there. Like, if I finish the chips, I might just move on to whatever is next in the pantry, even if it's not something I'd normally choose. I try to make good choices, really I do, but sometimes it's just easier to grab what's close. I hope that makes sense? I'm sorry if my answers are a little confusing...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed after these eating episodes, in addition to feeling gross and disappointed in yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. I just... I feel so gross and disappointed in myself afterward. Like, I'm an adult, I should have more control over this, you know? \n\nI try to keep a positive attitude most of the time, I really do. But after it happens, I just get really down on myself. I actually... um... I end up writing some pretty awful things about myself in my journal when I feel like that. I know I shouldn't, and I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. I hope you don't think less of me for that... I just get so frustrated with myself. Does that sound like what you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worth as a person is tied to your eating habits or your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to think like that. I know my weight doesn't, like, define me or make me a bad person. \n\nI guess I just... I want to be healthy and do a good job at work and everything. I know I get upset with myself sometimes, but I don't think I'm worthless just because of how I eat. I try to focus on the good things, you know? Like my sketching and trying to be a good person. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed with my weight or anything like that... Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to control it. But... I really try to reason with myself. But... I don't usually actually stop until the food is gone or I just feel too uncomfortable to keep going. I know that sounds bad, and I'm really trying to work on it. I hope you don't think I'm just weak-willed... is it normal to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. But my work uniforms still fit exactly the same, so I'm pretty sure it's been stable. \n\nI mean, maybe it fluctuates a pound or two here or there, but definitely nothing significant at all. I hope it's okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I can try to start weighing myself if that's something you need me to keep track of...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I know we just talked about this a little bit, and I really don't want you to think I'm completely helpless or anything... but, no, not really. \n\nLike I was saying, it just feels... automatic? Even when my brain is telling me to stop, like, \"Fumi, you really need to get back to your sketches,\" my hands just sort of keep going on their own. I don't really stop until everything is empty or my stomach is just too full and uncomfortable. I really do try to be in control, I promise. Is that okay? I mean, I hope that answers what you were asking without sounding too terrible...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during these episodes or is it more like you're eating whatever is available?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of a mix, I guess? I mean, I don't want to sound like I'm just blindly grabbing at things like a wild animal or something... I do usually start out picking something specific that I want. \n\nBut... I guess once I get going, it's more about just whatever is right there. Like, if I finish the chips, I might just move on to whatever is next in the pantry, even if it's not something I'd normally choose. I try to make good choices, really I do, but sometimes it's just easier to grab what's close. I hope that makes sense? I'm sorry if my answers are a little confusing...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed after these eating episodes, in addition to feeling gross and disappointed in yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. I just... I feel so gross and disappointed in myself afterward. Like, I'm an adult, I should have more control over this, you know? \n\nI try to keep a positive attitude most of the time, I really do. But after it happens, I just get really down on myself. I actually... um... I end up writing some pretty awful things about myself in my journal when I feel like that. I know I shouldn't, and I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. I hope you don't think less of me for that... I just get so frustrated with myself. Does that sound like what you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worth as a person is tied to your eating habits or your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to think like that. I know my weight doesn't, like, define me or make me a bad person. \n\nI guess I just... I want to be healthy and do a good job at work and everything. I know I get upset with myself sometimes, but I don't think I'm worthless just because of how I eat. I try to focus on the good things, you know? Like my sketching and trying to be a good person. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed with my weight or anything like that... Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to control it. But... I guess it's like, once I start, it feels like I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically? \n\nEven in my head, I'll be telling myself, like, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" or I'll remind myself that I need to get up early for work. I really try to reason with myself. But... I don't usually actually stop until the food is gone or I just feel too uncomfortable to keep going. I know that sounds bad, and I'm really trying to work on it. I hope you don't think I'm just weak-willed... is it normal to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I don't want it to sound like I'm just stuffing my face or anything, but... I eat really quickly, almost like I'm trying to get it all down before I have time to really think about what I'm doing. I barely even taste it, honestly. I know I should slow down and chew my food properly, and I'm really going to try to be better about that. I hope that doesn't make me sound too out of control... is it common for people to eat fast like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about these eating episodes throughout the day, even when you're not hungry?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. Um... yeah, I guess emotions do play a part. I don't want you to think I'm just eating because I'm sad or anything like that. I really try to manage my stress in healthy ways! 

But... well, a lot of the time when these episodes happen, I'm not even really hungry. Like, I might have already had dinner after my cleaning shift, so I shouldn't need more food. But if I'm just... really tired from being on my feet all day, or if I'm working on a design project for a class and get stuck on an idea... I just start opening snacks. And then I just keep going, even though I'm not hungry at all. 

I know I shouldn't eat when I'm not hungry, and I'm really trying to get better at recognizing when I'm actually full. Do you think... is it common for people to eat like that just because they're tired or stuck on something? I really want to learn how to stop doing that.

And then I just keep going, even though I'm not hungry at all. \n\nI know I shouldn't eat when I'm not hungry, and I'm really trying to get better at recognizing when I'm actually full. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um... I guess so? I mean, I really don't want it to sound like I'm just gorging myself or completely out of control, because I'm not! I really do try to just get a normal dinner. 

But sometimes, on those nights after my cleaning shift... I'll stop at the convenience store. And I'll tell myself I'm just getting dinner, but... then I end up getting a big bowl of ramen, and maybe several rice balls, and some fried chicken... and then some sweets, too. And then I go back to my room and I just... I end up eating all of it within an hour or two. 

I know it's probably more than most people would eat, and I really shouldn't buy that much at once. I try so hard to just buy the ramen! But it just sort of happens before I realize it. Is... is it really bad that I eat that much? I really am trying to make better choices at the store.

I hope it's okay that I don't have an exact number for you? I can try to start weighing myself if that's something you need me to keep track of...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of what and how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I hope that answers what you were asking without sounding too terrible...\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to choose what you eat during these episodes or is it more like you're eating whatever is available?\n\nPatient: Um... well, it's kind of a mix, I guess? Like, if I finish the chips, I might just move on to whatever is next in the pantry, even if it's not something I'd normally choose. I try to make good choices, really I do, but sometimes it's just easier to grab what's close. I hope that makes sense? I'm sorry if my answers are a little confusing...\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or depressed after these eating episodes, in addition to feeling gross and disappointed in yourself?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, that's actually kind of why I came in to see you. I just... I feel so gross and disappointed in myself afterward. Like, I'm an adult, I should have more control over this, you know? \n\nI try to keep a positive attitude most of the time, I really do. But after it happens, I just get really down on myself. I actually... um... I end up writing some pretty awful things about myself in my journal when I feel like that. I know I shouldn't, and I'm trying to be better about it, I promise. I hope you don't think less of me for that... I just get so frustrated with myself. Does that sound like what you were asking about?\n\nDoctor: Do you think your worth as a person is tied to your eating habits or your weight?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try really hard not to think like that. I know my weight doesn't, like, define me or make me a bad person. \n\nI guess I just... I want to be healthy and do a good job at work and everything. I know I get upset with myself sometimes, but I don't think I'm worthless just because of how I eat. I try to focus on the good things, you know? Like my sketching and trying to be a good person. I hope that's the right way to look at it? I really don't want to sound like I'm obsessed with my weight or anything like that... Is that okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're able to stop eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I try really hard to stop, I promise I do. I don't want you to think I'm just giving up or not trying to control it. But... I guess it's like, once I start, it feels like I just keep reaching for the next thing almost automatically? \n\nEven in my head, I'll be telling myself, like, \"Fumi, stop, you have sketches to finish,\" or I'll remind myself that I need to get up early for work. I really try to reason with myself. But... I don't usually actually stop until the food is gone or I just feel too uncomfortable to keep going. I know that sounds bad, and I'm really trying to work on it. I hope you don't think I'm just weak-willed... is it normal to feel like that sometimes?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating much more quickly than usual during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess I do. I mean, I don't want it to sound like I'm just stuffing my face or anything, but... I do tend to eat pretty fast when it happens. \n\nIt's usually when I'm just standing at the kitchen counter, or sometimes I'm sitting on my bed and my sketchbook is still open next to me. I just... I eat really quickly, almost like I'm trying to get it all down before I have time to really think about what I'm doing. I barely even taste it, honestly. I know I should slow down and chew my food properly, and I'm really going to try to be better about that. I hope that doesn't make me sound too out of control... is it common for people to eat fast like that?\n\nDoctor: Do you often find yourself thinking about these eating episodes throughout the day, even when you're not hungry?\n\nPatient: Um... no, not really. I mean, I try to keep my mind on other things, you know? Like my work, or what I'm going to draw next. \n\nI don't want you to think I'm just sitting around obsessing over food all day. I really try to stay busy and productive. Sometimes a thought might pop up, like if I walk past the breakroom at work or something, but I try to just push it away and focus on my job. I really want to be doing the right things and not letting this take over my life. I hope that's a good sign? That I'm not, like, fixated on it all the time?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're in control of how much you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Um... I mean, I really try to be in control. I don't want you to think I'm just completely letting go or not caring. I do care, a lot. \n\nBut... well, during those times, I guess it feels like my control is just... slipping a little bit? Like, I'll tell myself I'm only going to have a little, and I really mean it when I say it. But then it just sort of happens, and before I know it, I've eaten more than I meant to. I try so hard to pull it back, I really do. I don't want to be someone who just can't control themselves. Do you think... do you think the fact that I'm trying so hard means I can get better at it?\n\nDoctor: Have these eating episodes been happening for more than 3 months?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess it has been a while. I really wish I didn't do it, and I always tell myself I'm going to stop. But I can't really remember a recent month where I went more than a week or so without it happening. I even end up writing some pretty awful things about myself in my journal. I know I shouldn't do that, and I'm really trying to be more positive, I promise! But it's just hard in the moment. \n\nIs it normal to get that down on yourself? I just really want to fix it and do the right thing.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually have these eating episodes when you're feeling stressed or emotional?\n\nPatient: Um... yeah, I guess emotions do play a part. I really do try to just get a normal dinner. \n\nBut sometimes, on those nights after my cleaning shift... I'll stop at the convenience store. And I'll tell myself I'm just getting dinner, but... then I end up getting a big bowl of ramen, and maybe several rice balls, and some fried chicken... and then some sweets, too. And then I go back to my room and I just... I end up eating all of it within an hour or two. \n\nI know it's probably more than most people would eat, and I really shouldn't buy that much at once.
